  • Chapter 650: Barbarian Business (3)

A wild mane covered his face as if he wore a wig.

And through that tangled hair, one eye was visible.

Thump-!

His height was about six meters.

Although well beyond the standard for large monsters, his perceived size was even greater thanks to his overly long arms.

Well, he was probably still smaller than Hiphramagent.

Thump-!

Still, the pressure he radiated was on another level.

That was because I knew that guy better than anyone else.

The Lord of Silence, Silriat.

The fifth-floor Floor Lord armed with tricky gimmicks.

To me, an ‘explorer’ who uses essences, he was a calamity.

Like when I faced ‘the traitor’ Ricardo Rühenpraha who possessed his essence, it was impossible to counter with ‘binding’ magic...

‘Why is he here?’

Despite the question, my actions were immediate.

Tap-tap.

The moment I locked eyes with him, I stepped back and pulled Elwen close.

And then...

“...!”

Replacing my voice that wouldn’t come out, I gave a simple signal.

At the same time, I looked at the mage Gahwin who met my gaze.

‘Attack.’

‘Very strongly.’

If the clan Anabada members were here, I could have sent more complex signals, but whining wouldn’t change anything.

Fortunately, Gahwin immediately obeyed the order.

「Gahwin Vesillus cast the ancient spell [Katuka’s Thunderstorm].」

Katuka’s Thunderstorm.

One of the ancient spells Gahwin, the ‘mage’ role, could cast, an instant spell with no casting time.

Naturally, its power was far from lacking.

Flash!

Several flashes like the sky was collapsing covered the entire canyon.

Ruuuuum!

Not a sound, but a massive vibration struck the skin.

And simultaneously,

「Character has exited the range of [Silent Curse].」

The Lord of Silence, Silriat, who was climbing the cliff, fell down and the sounds returned.

“Ugh...”

Gahwin, who had used the spell first, had a somewhat dumbfounded look.

“Hmm... a fascinating monster.”

The village chief was unperturbed.

Ah, now that I think about it, maybe there were no Floor Lords in the Labyrinth in this man’s time?

According to the records engraved on the Stone of Honor, Floor Lords began appearing in the Labyrinth only after the Abyss Seeker Remenin reached the 10th floor.

“Lord Vesillus, do you know the identity of that monster?”

“If I saw correctly... the creature we just saw is the Floor Lord of Daemagyeong...”

“Floor Lord...?”

The village chief tilted his head, unfamiliar with the term.

But there was no time for a kind explanation.

“What are you waiting for! Follow me quickly!”

First, we had to get out of here.

Even if we had to kill that guy someday, this narrow canyon wasn’t a good place to fight him.

Besides, I needed time to organize my thoughts.

“Elwen, hold on tight.”

“Yes, yes!”

I pulled Elwen close and quickly moved forward.

Tap-tap.

As we started running seriously, I noticed another change. No matter how the monsters appeared, we had tried to break through somehow.

‘Maybe a small mercy...’

Perhaps due to the appearance of the Lord of Silence, the monsters blocking the path vanished without a trace.

“Baron, at least tell me where we’re going!”

“Downward.”

“Downward...? But the Floor Lord will be waiting below!”

“That’s why we’re going. If we fight, flat ground is better than the canyon.”

“Are you seriously planning to fight him?”

Gahwin shouted as if ★ Novelight ★ it was absurd, but I only had one thing to say.

“Then is there any other way?”

“Damn it, a Floor Lord! The kind that kills people even when hundreds prepare to fight! And you want five people to take him down? And that fairy here can’t even pull her own weight?”

It wasn’t a reaction I couldn’t understand.

The Floor Lord’s infamous reputation was well known.

The harsh words were probably because of the field effect.

Since I had no time to treat wounds with the hammer, I decided to let it slide this time—

“Gahwin Vesillus!”

At that moment, the village chief’s shout echoed through the canyon.

I had never heard the chief’s voice so stern.

“Y-Yes...?”

Gahwin, completely stunned, looked at the chief, who spoke firmly.

“Stop complaining and follow the Baron’s orders. This is an order.”

“Eh? B-But...”

“Do you intend to disobey?”

“N-No... I just thought there might be another way besides fighting him—”

“Answer yes or no only. Be careful though. Your answer today will determine your future.”

“...I will obey.”

Thanks to the chief stepping in on time, the situation calmed quickly.

If command wavered at a time like this, there’d be no hope. Fortunately, the chief seemed aware of that.

Tap-tap.

The chief then increased speed and approached my side at the front.

“Judging from that guy’s reaction, he seems a very tough enemy. But I guess he has some sense?”

“...Of course.”

More precisely, he was still thinking.

Anyway, it was not yet a situation for despair.

The essentials for the raid were already in place.

‘Priest, mage, knight.’

The most important human resources for raiding the Lord of Silence. Though five people was still too few...

Unlimited holy power and ancient magic.

The chief also showed overwhelming presence all chapter, maybe due to his inflated stats.

That met the minimum conditions.

‘The problem is me and Elwen...’

Still, thinking positively, there’s a high chance of some ‘role’ in the raid.

The [Dungeon & Stone] game I know was like that.

‘When the boss fight starts, we’ll run the raid with those three and coordinate.’

The most important here is the priest.

If he uses infinite holy power well, survival is possible—

“N-Nooo-!! Stop right there!!”

Suddenly there was a commotion behind.

Stopping and turning around, I saw Gahwin and the archbishop already stopped.

Well, more precisely...

“The archbishop suddenly stopped and isn’t moving!”

Gahwin looked at the archbishop with concern as if he was entranced.

“Step aside for a moment.”

I put down Elwen and approached to check, but the archbishop just stared blankly into the void.

Something was clearly wrong.

‘What is this?’

The Lord of Silence didn’t have such a pattern.

Is it because we’re inside the fissure?

If so, that adds too many variables—

“Baron.”

Suddenly the archbishop spoke.

‘Whoa, that startled me...’

While quietly soothing my shocked heart, I calmly checked the situation.

“Archbishop, are you alright? What just happened?”

If it was a problem, we needed to urgently find a solution—

“Don’t worry. I’m fine. Actually, I’m in better condition than ever.”

What now?

“...What does that mean?”

“Literally. The miracle of God fills my entire body. I can’t remember ever feeling so fulfilled in my life...”

Judging by his eyes, the archbishop was still a mess.

Damn, there’s no time to waste.

“Baron.”

As I was pondering how to handle the archbishop, he shifted his gaze to look at me.

“Have you ever thought about serving something other than your ancestors’ spirits?”

The same words he said to me before the Lord of Silence appeared. But this time, the moment I heard it, I felt an unease.

Not sure yet, but...

“I’m asking just in case.”

I needed to confirm.

“The ‘other something’ you mentioned—is it Reatlas?”

The archbishop was silent.

But as if answering for him,

Swishhhhhhh—

Thousands of black particles rose from the darkness of the Labyrinth. They gathered around the archbishop like a blessing.

Unfortunately, I knew the identity of that effect.

It was the effect when becoming Karui’s priest.

Swishhhhhhhhhh—

The archbishop smiled brightly and reached out toward the black particles.

Denying it any further was meaningless.

“It’s visible, isn’t it? The grace of God?”

The archbishop had become Karui’s priest.

「Eden Hestia has succumbed to temptation.」

「You can now use Karui’s powers.」

My mind went blank.

Suddenly, the priest of our team became Karui’s priest.

What should I do?

‘Should I kill him right now?’

Looking at his defenseless state, it didn’t seem entirely impossible.

No, I even thought there might be no better chance.

I had to decide quickly.

If the archbishop with infinite holy power turned enemy after becoming Karui’s priest, it would be a big problem.

‘But... if I kill him, then what?’

Putting aside moral doubts about killing the archbishop so easily.

Without a priest, the Floor Lord was impossible to defeat.

Yet my head was in turmoil.

‘Maybe I shouldn’t kill him.’

I still didn’t know the strategy for this fissure.

But if the archbishop turned priest played a key role?

An impulsive choice could bring irreversible consequences—

「Character cast [Swing].」

When I came to my senses, my hammer was already swinging toward the archbishop’s head.

Not a carefully considered decision, but an instant judgment.

Crack!

With all my strength, my hammer struck and half of the archbishop’s head flew off.

But he still stood.

I wasn’t surprised since I knew Karui’s priests could enter half-head mode.

“W-What... are you doing!!”

But maybe Gahwin, who watched, was different.

“A-Attacking the archbishop! Are you truly insane!!”

Ah. He just didn’t understand the situation.

There was no time to explain.

‘One more time.’

I swung the hammer again, but unfortunately, tentacles growing from the archbishop’s body blocked it.

The sight was disgusting and unsettling, hardly holy magic.

“W-What on earth is going on?!”

I struck a few more times but gave up after the tentacles counterattacked and briefly explained.

“The archbishop has become Karui’s priest.”

“What?”

“Look. Even with half his head blown off, he’s still alive.”

“T-Then shouldn’t we finish him off quickly?”

That same guy who scolded me for attacking the archbishop earlier.

“Count Saintred, will you try?”

After quickly issuing the command, the chief drew aura and swung his sword without objection.

But...

Kraaaang!

Even the aura was blocked by that shell.

Yet the chief showed no disappointment.

“If I go all out, it might be possible... but after that, I’ll have to be considered gone. It’s a finishing move. Do you want me to do that?”

The chief looked at me as if everything else was my choice.

That deepened my dilemma.

Since he was so sure, he’d probably keep his word...

‘If even the chief disappears, only the mage will remain.’

I had to consider what would come next—

“You all...”

At that moment, the archbishop’s mouth opened.

“You still don’t understand me at all.”

His voice was void of any emotion, like the chief’s former tone.

“Don’t worry. Soon you will understand.”

As the archbishop’s ominous declaration ended,

His chest, bent backward like receiving a command ritual, opened wide.

Kakakakakakak!

I recognized the skill immediately.

「Eden Hestia cast [Unstable Vessel].」

Unstable Vessel.

Karui priest’s strongest finishing skill.

The effect was simple.

For the casting time, he becomes temporarily invincible...

‘Then explodes.’

In terms of range and power, no skill in [Dungeon & Stone] could match it.

‘It’s already too late to dodge.’

I made a decision.

Even if I had to give up on the chief, I had to stop that first.

Just as I was about to speak with that thought,

Thump-!

Vibrations from the ground erased all sound.

「Character has entered the range of [Silent Curse].」

On top of everything, the Floor Lord I had dropped earlier appeared again in this situation...

‘Attack.’

‘Very strongly.’

I urgently sent the same signal I gave Gahwin to the chief. But the chief just shook his head with a troubled expression.

Ah... maybe essences can’t be used when sealed?

‘Damn it.’

So I hurriedly changed the signal.

‘Run with all your might.’

‘Anywhere is fine.’

‘Survive.’

A survival signal I was using for the first time in a long explorer life.

Once I confirmed everyone understood the signals, I lifted Elwen and stood at the cliff’s edge.

Of course, I had no certainty.

Would we survive if we fell here?

‘Still... it’s more likely than getting hit by that skill.’

Just as I was about to push off to leap,

Thump-!

A huge hand appeared below the cliff.

I tried to jump without caring, but the hand’s movement was strange.

It didn’t seem to be aiming for us.

‘Uh...?'

The creature draped its upper body over the path and crouched as if hugging the archbishop tightly.

And...

“...”

For a moment our eyes met.

Then.

Plop.

It relaxed its grip, holding the archbishop, and fell down the cliff.

「Character has exited the range of [Silent Curse].」

About three seconds later, the distance grew and [Silent Curse] was lifted.

And after some time.

Kwaaaaaaaaaaaang!

An explosion like a nuclear bomb erupted from below.

“Everyone! Hold tight!!”

Daemagyeong began to collapse.

Gripping Elwen tightly in preparation for the fall—

Boom—!

The ground beneath our feet collapsed, and a sensation of floating surged through my whole body. But it was distinctly different from the feeling of falling.

Whoooooosh—!

Defying gravity, my body rose above the crumbled earth.

It was a levitation spell.

“Everyone, are you alright?”

I quickly scanned around and saw Gahun and the Chief also floating in midair.

This time, I praised sincerely.

“Thanks to you, we survived. Well done.”

I was so flustered that I couldn’t even give orders properly, yet they reacted like this on their own.

“...It’s not something to be praised for.”

Ha, the old man is embarrassed.

Maybe because we were bickering just a moment ago and now he feels awkward?

I didn’t know, but first things first—I needed to confirm something.

“How long can the magic last?”

“It should be enough until we land safely.”

Alright, that means...

“Good. Then from now on, descend slowly... no, descend quickly enough not to get seriously hurt.”

I decided this because if something happened while levitating, proper resistance would be impossible.

Once I gave the order, Gahun finally regained his senses and began descending.

Ka-BOOM! Crash! Rumble rumble rumble...

The surroundings were literally like a hellscape.

Everything visible was collapsing and crumbling, with huge falling rocks threateningly rushing past us.

And then...

“Now it finally feels like an exploration.”

The Chief, while descending like falling, looked around with eyes full of curiosity.

It was probably the first time I’d seen such lively eyes on him.

That guy always seemed a bit off his rocker, but I had no time to care about him.

“What was that just now?”

The Hierarch had grabbed the Archbishop moments before a massive explosion and dove underground.

That guy, who I thought was our enemy, somehow acted like he was helping us...

“Elwen, did you control him? Tell him to hold the Archbishop and fall?”

The first possibility I checked was this, but unfortunately, it wasn’t the case.

“No. I tried, but it didn’t work.”

Huh, so the Hierarch acted on his own?

“Maybe that monster wasn’t our enemy after all.”

The Chief must have heard our conversation because he muttered something.

Well, it wasn’t entirely wrong.

When I saw the Hierarch, I thought he must be the boss monster.

But...

“Thinking back now, maybe he was an ally.”

Like the Hamshik we met in Chapter 2.

More than that, higher beings seemed to show favorable behavior toward Elwen, which was a hint.

Ka-BANG! Boom!

As I mulled over this during the descent—

Ka-Booooooooooom!

A giant boulder slammed down on Gahun’s head.

It was a dizzying situation, but thanks to the protective shield we’d spread out in preparation for falling rocks, not a scratch was made.

But the problem was...

“Ah...”

That ‘ah’ sounded so ominous.

Sure enough, bad feelings rarely miss.

“What happened?”

“Th-the levitation spell’s formula just broke.”

“The formula broke? Explain that in a way I can understand—”

Do it.

Just as I was about to say more—

“Ah...”

Our descent stopped, we briefly hovered—

“Kyaaaaaaah—!”

And we fell straight down.

My head aches.

My lungs feel stabbed by something, suffocating, and my spine won’t move properly.

But...

‘I’m alive.’

Then what about the others?

The last thing I remember was seeing the ground while falling, clutching Elwen, and taking the impact on my back...

“...Ah, ah... damn it! Damn it! My words... Riese...?”

Elwen’s urgent voice crackled like a broken radio inside my ringing ears.

I also heard the Chief’s voice.

“Don’t move... wait... I’ve given you potions, so soon...”

“Sir! Sir!”

“They’re not responding at all...”

Feeling voices gradually becoming clearer, I slowly lifted my eyelids.

“Sir...!”

“I’m... okay... stop.”

“Ah, ah! Sorry!”

Only then did Elwen get off me, making it a bit easier to breathe.

“Hold on a bit more. Judging from your recovery, you’ll be able to move soon.”

“How long was I out?”

“About five minutes.”

“And Gahun...?”

Trying to grasp the situation, I asked, and the Chief pointed somewhere.

Turning my stiff neck # Nоvеlight # sideways, I saw Gahun lying with broken legs.

“He’s not dead. Looks like he used magic at the end to slow the speed as much as possible. I gave him potions, but I don’t know when he’ll wake up.”

Right, that’s what I thought...

“And you?”

“I’m fine. Kept my senses until the end and minimized the impact properly.”

Lucky.

If only my defense stats had been intact, my body wouldn’t be this wrecked.

“...The enemies?”

“For now, no one’s around, so focus on recovering without worries.”

“Understood. But your tone...”

“Well, I’m older and higher rank anyway, aren’t I? We went through a life-or-death situation together, so I can speak comfortably.”

Well, that’s true.

Even if his tone changed suddenly, no one around would really suspect.

“Then I’ll rest a bit more...”

After that, I closed my eyes and lay down, and before long, I recovered enough to move on my own.

“Want some water?”

“Ah, thanks.”

I drank a glass and got up. The Chief asked like he’d been waiting.

“So what’s the plan now?”

Since the Halfwit Team days to the Anavada Clan, whenever something happened, that was the line.

People always ask me for answers. It used to be frustrating, but now I just see it as my fate.

I also don’t want to pass all responsibility to others and just follow orders.

“Will we wait until Sir Vesilrus wakes up?”

Well, I don’t know what’s best either.

It’s the first time this kind of rift has appeared.

From its unusual progression to the Hierarch priest Karui...

Honestly, it’s headache-inducing.

But...

“No, first I’ll take care of Gahun and start searching the surroundings.”

Let’s do what we can.

If we drag things out, the situation might get worse.

“I’ll take care of Elwen and Gahun, Count, you lead the way.”

“That I will.”

Though it was the most dangerous mission, the Chief obediently followed my order.

Not because he thought he was most suitable for the task.

“Keep your promise.”

For helping in this rift, I promised to bring ‘Karui’s Heart’ to the city.

“Don’t worry. I have no intention of breaking my promise.”

“That’s a relief. Also, if things go wrong later, check your notebook.”

“Notebook?”

“I put it in your backpack when I gave it to you. It has everything you might be curious about, so it will help on the journey.”

I didn’t know there was a notebook.

I got the backpack just before entering the rift. I even left it outside because the Chief suggested, just in case.

‘I’ll check the notebook as soon as we get out of here...’

We moved for quite some time over the broken ruins.

“...”

“...”

At some point, the sounds ceased, and silence arrived.

Now, no one was unaware of the reason for this phenomenon.

“The character has entered the [Silent Curse]’s range.”

“All essence skills are sealed.”

A Hierarch is nearby.

Should we avoid it then?

I didn’t know the answer, but I gave a signal to the Chief, who seemed to be waiting for orders.

‘Continue.’

Since I knew nothing about this chapter, we had to get information by direct encounter...

‘It might even be an ally.’

Even if an enemy, it was unlikely normal after hugging the Archbishop right before the explosion and falling.

“...”

After continuing straight without changing direction, we found the Lord of Silence, Silierat.

“...”

“...”

In the suffocating silence, the creature was half-buried in rock.

As expected, it was not in good condition.

Of its three exposed arms, two had bones showing, and it had no strength to move, blinking its eyelids weakly while buried in the debris.

“...”

Relieved that it posed no threat, yet a chill ran down my spine.

I already knew its power, but to have a Hierarch reduced to such a state with one blow...

If we’d been caught in that explosion head-on, what would have happened?

“The Archbishop... isn’t here.”

I scanned the area, then gently put Elwen down and slowly approached it.

Lord of Silence, Silierat.

It didn’t move even as I neared.

It just looked at me with tired eyes.

“...”

Reaching out cautiously, I touched its face.

It remained quiet even on contact.

As if it had no reason to be hostile.

‘Why did you help us?’

I wanted to ask, but no voice came out.

Even if I could speak, I doubted it would give a proper answer.

So I answered myself.

‘...Yeah, you must have your reasons too.’

Now I know something about this labyrinth.

Rift guardians, Hierarchs, were real beings with their own stories.

Maybe not just them, but all monsters here are the same.

In a way, they’re even more pitiful than me, dragged into this strange world and causing havoc.

I withdrew my hand and stepped back; the Chief gave me a signal.

‘Kill it.’

A simple gesture with that meaning.

I quietly shook my head and made a gesture to step back.

“...?”

The Chief looked puzzled but obeyed, perhaps because of our promise.

As soon as we left its territory—

“Why didn’t you kill it?”

The Chief asked.

“Seeing it like that, it didn’t seem dangerous.”

I’m not sure.

Why did I make that choice?

“Because it helped us for now. Killing it hastily might cause problems. We can kill it after getting more info.”

What I told the Chief wasn’t a complete lie.

But...

“Hmm, that could be true.”

Unlike the Chief who accepted easily, I was confused.

The Hierarch’s essence.

The Hierarch’s relic.

The Hierarch’s stone used to open rifts.

Maybe killing it would have given us those rewards easily.

But I didn’t.

‘It’s definitely not sympathy...’

Hard to explain.

I can only say it was an impulsive decision—

[Come here...]

Then, once again, an unknown voice echoed, ending my thoughts.

The sound came from the exact opposite direction from where the Hierarch was.

So one thing was clear.

‘That sound wasn’t from it...’

The weird dialogue that called us like a pervert to lure us wasn’t from the Hierarch.

Well, it was odd since the in-game character had no lines.

“Then we just head toward where the sound is coming from?”

I nodded; the Chief took the lead.

Following with Elwen at some distance, soon we saw the source of the voice directly.

[Right...]

[Yes, come here and hold me...]

It was a completely unexpected existence.

“Soul Eater...?”

A Grade 5 rare species.

Only one spawns in the Great Demon Mirror when the labyrinth opens, granting between 100 to 200 experience randomly when defeated.

[I will grant you the wish you desire...]

What is this?

Wasn’t this supposed to be the boss monster or something?

“Soul Eater has summoned [Soul Cavalry].”

It seemed the skill was basically the same as the original...

[Kyaaaaaaah—!!]

The Soul Eater was cut in half by the Chief’s roughly swung sword and defeated immediately.

“Soul Eater defeated.”

Up to here, it was surprising but no problem.

Like a MacGuffin in a novel.

Something that looks important but actually is just a normal mob.

You could interpret it that way and move on.

But...

‘What the hell is this now?’

The following scene left me dumbfounded again.

[Guardian Defeat Bonus. EXP +3]

A rainbow-colored essence meaning ‘guardian’ floated in the air where it had been.

Whoooosh—!

A portal appeared, indicating the rift was cleared.

Red and blue.

For some reason, there were two of them.

When the guardian is defeated, the rift clears and a portal opens. Riding that portal allows return to the original entry point.

Well, at least that’s how it had been so far.

“Two portals...?”

And they’re even colored red and blue.

This isn’t some kind of pill.

“If we pick the wrong one, do we end up somewhere strange...?”

Unexpected situations made me think a lot.

Somehow, nothing on this underground first floor ever proceeds normally.

Really feels like everything is shrouded in mystery.

‘Including this essence...’

The portals opened after clearing the rift don’t disappear over time.

Though, whether this rule properly applies here is uncertain.

Anyway, I briefly took my eyes off the portals and examined the essence. freewebnovel.com

“A truly mysterious color...”

The Chief’s eyes were still full of curiosity.

Well, since he was from a time before Hierarchs and rifts, it was probably his first time seeing a rainbow-colored essence.

“Baron, can you explain what kind of essence this is?”

“I don’t know.”

“Oh, so even you are seeing this monster for the first time?”

“No, that’s not it...”

Feeling frustrated, I sighed deeply and continued.

“The Soul Eater normally doesn’t drop essences.”

Not that I just haven’t gotten one yet.

Soul Eater is a monster that gives a huge amount of experience. Even if skipping other monsters, I always hunted this one when leveling a new character.

But not once has it dropped an essence.

Even with Beginner’s Luck, Melton’s Soul Clover, and max essence drop rate buffs, the result was the same.

So I thought it simply doesn’t drop essence...

‘Didn’t expect there’d be another way to obtain it like this.’

Then what’s the effect of the Soul Eater essence?

At least I expect it to include the active skill to summon ‘Soul Cavalry’...

But I’ll have to experiment after acquiring it.

Oh, of course, I don’t plan to consume it myself.

“Gahun! Get up!”

I put down Gahun, who had been carried on my shoulder, and gently slapped his cheek. Since the essence dropped, there was no time to wait leisurely.

The essence must be placed in a test tube within 30 minutes.

“Gahun! Gahun! Gahun!”

So I increased the slapping intensity gradually.

It wasn’t comfortable to do this to someone who had collapsed in pain...

But essence comes before people, right?

“Ugh...”

Luckily, by the time his cheek had swollen, Gahun regained consciousness.

“Ba...ron? I’m... dizzy...”

He seemed still dizzy from the fall’s impact as he spoke vaguely.

I briefly said the essentials.

Handling mages is similar anyway.

“While you were unconscious, the Soul Eater appeared as a guardian, and after killing it, guardian essence dropped.”

“W-what...?!”

A legion commander is always busy.

Because a legion commander has no room for excuses.

That’s why...

The current expedition’s composition was simple.

We are in a labyrinth.

Mistakes would happen repeatedly until they got skilled.

The most efficient way to manage subordinates is to place suitable supervisors above and to push them hard.

The so-called downward pressure method passed down since ancient armies.

So, I deleted all existing leadership meeting members from the records and rebuilt the structure from scratch.

Mages, clerics, ranged, melee, knights, tanks, support, non-combatants.

Separating all personnel by class regardless of squad, then selecting one responsible leader per class and assigning appropriate titles.

I just made up titles as I went.

The names themselves didn’t really matter.

For example, among mages: research, investigation, combat.

Court Mage.

Oh, so it has a cooldown like an item?

Simply put, the guardian’s grade is determined by the grade of crack stone offered to the statue.

Well, this is still speculation.

We checked our crack stones, but unfortunately, only a few below grade 6 remained.

Understandable, how often do you bring crack stones into a labyrinth?

Actually, having even that many proved how meticulous the royal family was.

By the way, among the Numbers items, there is one similar to this statue.

No.18 Crack Generator.

This item can open cracks by inserting crack stones.

It requires about ten grade 9 stones and opens random cracks on the corresponding floor.

Though you have to be at that floor, it can open cracks without high-grade stones if you have enough quantity. And it actually enters the real cracks, so it’s an upper-level item.

The expedition’s research chief, Raven, activated voice control magic.

Imagining a giant barbarian wearing a crown looks odd, but gamers always prioritize function over looks...

As a tank who needs periodic healing, this helmet isn’t for me.

99.9% of the currency in this world is released into circulation through the Dimension Plaza checkpoint.

A plan for fleeing when facing overwhelming enemies or trials.

Crack!

Due to the rainy season’s nature, fierce battles were unavoidable early on, so sacrifices were inevitable.

Two deaths...

Well, unlucky deaths happen even fighting goblins in this line of work.

‘But... is this really the end?’

The peak of the rainy season had passed and was slowly weakening.

Perhaps because of that?

A poison of carelessness began to loosen inside.

Accidents happen more when confidence is moderately high than when a beginner.

Crack!

For barbarians, a single blanket is enough.

The dimensional disruption phenomenon is basically a ‘barrier.’

Like at the witch’s hut or the blood-red fortress.

Though the portal leads you inside what looks like another world, a surrounding barrier is in place that can’t be broken no matter what you do.

It’s more likely that you enter by fulfilling some condition.

No insects were found in the jungle.

Oh, I didn’t know that.

The [One-Eyed] effect that enhances vision doesn’t consume resources, so there’s no need to remove it.

Hearts.

Raven was standing dazed on the rail, being moved.

Toward the device that transformed monsters into magic stones.

A device that turns monsters into magic stones.

What happens if a human is put into that device?

I don’t have data on that yet.

Thud—!

The moment I saw Raven, I pushed off the ground and shot forward.

“Ugh...”

What, is this working?

“Ugh...”

Thunk! Thunk!

“St... stop...”

Raven’s condition seemed to improve as he muttered barely formed words.

Maybe the condition to break the charm requires a certain amount of damage.

To the place with the device that turns monsters into magic stones.

Monsters transformed into fist-sized magic stones by the beam.

Damn it.

Thump—!

In Dungeon and Stone, that sometimes happened.

Countless buttons and levers.

Buttons below the screen affect the camera position of that screen.

‘Pressing red stops the rails; green restarts them; pulling the left lever reverses the rails...’

Pulling the right lever changed the screen view to control another area. Each button and lever had various uses.

Unlike others attached below the screens, these were alone in the room center, so we couldn’t check them lightly.

Who doesn’t know that?

Escaping this temple is easy.

Just exit where I came in.

With agreement, no need to hesitate.

Not an AI or anything.

How should I take this mysterious presence trying to talk beyond the screen?

Oddly, the phrase resembled what Captain Baekin said.

The difference: Baekin spoke uncertainly, but the Director sounded confident.

At the Director’s question, Hamsik trembled.

[Yes, I want to know.]

At Hamsik’s reply, the light cluster on the screen began moving.

[You are currently in a research facility named Panthelion. It was a secret underground institution operated by the Empire.]

Though Hamsik said he had been here before, I wondered why this story was starting now.

[The Empire established this lab for one purpose: to create loyal and powerful biological weapons stronger than any soldier.]

[Many mages, scholars, and alchemists gathered to study all knowledge and conduct experiments. Yet, results were always insufficient.]

[This changed when witches appeared. An unprecedented war broke out, and the Empire was under threat.]

[All talent was gathered to the lab, and unprecedented support was given. Most importantly, new test subjects increased.]

[Thousands of monsters, including those created by witches, were brought in daily. Panthelion’s research made tremendous progress in a short time.]

The light cluster flickered wildly as if opening eyes.

[One of those test subjects was you, Marx.]

After locking subordinate G in the basement and drawing the curtains, we gathered in the dark living room to freely exchange opinions.

“...The situation isn’t good,” Versil was the first to speak.

“Soon the patrol that discovered us will notice we’re gone. Then a full-scale search will begin. It’s only a matter of time before our presence here is exposed.”

Indeed, staying here any longer is dangerous.

Even for us, being surrounded by them would mean no chance of escape.

I had felt that during the Ice Rock expedition as well; the high-ranking plunderers of Noirak, including Orculus, were not to be underestimated.

‘Since we continued to assault the 8th floor afterward, they must have become even more elite.’

I nodded at Versil’s opinion and said,

“Don’t worry. I have no intention of hiding here either. We must find a way to escape as soon as possible.”

“That’s a relief.”

We must escape the 7th district occupied by the Noirak forces.

With that clear goal, the conversation naturally focused on that.

“Not only the 7th, but the 13th district is occupied at the same time, right? It would be difficult for them to fully cover such a vast city wall alone.”

“Meaning we should find gaps and break through.”

“Yes! Once we get over the wall, our allies will be beyond it.”

Elwen insisted on a forced breakthrough.

“How about using the sewers...? The passages are narrow, so even if we get caught, it seems doable...”

Misha suggested a detour.

“...Why bother with all that? Why not just hide? The royal troops might come to rescue us in a few days...”

Ainard’s wait-it-out strategy, lacking fighting spirit.

Then...

“Auyen, what about you? Don’t just stay quiet.”

“Huh? Me?”

“I’m not asking for the perfect answer. Just say freely what you would want to do. No pressure.”

“If I had the strength... I think I would try to rescue the people.”

“People...?”

“Yes... Most of the explorers in the 7th district are being held hostage by them...”

Auyen’s opinion was surprisingly thoughtful.

“Of course, I’m far from being a hero...! Just saying, don’t mind me!”

Flustered by all eyes on him, Auyen added hurriedly.

But...

“Not bad.”

Amelia, apparently impressed by Auyen’s opinion, looked at me and said,

“Yandel, this might be our chance. They don’t know we exist. They’re only wary of the royal troops beyond the city walls.”

“So you mean we should strike from inside during this moment.”

“Exactly. If successful, it will be a tremendous achievement. Two cities of Ravigion are under external enemy control now. We might be recognized with as much honor as the nobles who stopped the Duke of Yeokcheon during the Cheolbyeok Rebellion.”

Amelia’s words made sense.

Such a large incident in Lapdonia’s long history can be counted on one hand.

Success would mean numerous rewards and even noble rank advancement.

My status in the Melbes coalition of other-race nobles would rise accordingly.

“...We should listen more. Auyen, bring the guy from the basement.”

Our worries deepened.

Subordinate G, briefly imprisoned in the basement and then freed, answered our questions as if he had truly joined our side.

Thanks to him, we learned much more detailed information about the 7th district’s situation...

“The explorers are imprisoned in several locations.”

By profession, explorers greatly surpass ordinary people in combat ability and usually act as the city’s standing army.

The places they were imprisoned could be roughly divided into three:

The underground prison of the explorers’ branch, where I had been once trapped.

The underground prison beneath the Sanseonggyo temples.

And the underground prison of the 7th district police.

Of course, these three are broad divisions; subdivided further, there are dozens of places.

There are several temples and explorer branches in the 7th district, and the police have multiple sub-branches.

“Because so many explorers were taken hostage, they had no choice but to split them up like this...”

Whether this is good or bad is ambiguous.

‘Their forces are dispersed, which might make rescue easier...’

On the other hand, stopping by even a few places would surely expose our existence. Even moving quickly, there is distance between prisons.

“But it’s positive that their strength increases with every place we visit.”

“Eh...? Are you planning to rescue them...?”

“Why do you care?”

“......”

Anyway, we then heard about the patrols’ territories and the size of troops stationed along the city walls.

Well, this low-ranking lackey couldn’t explain everything precisely...

“Oh, and civilians are gathered near the walls. As the royal pressure grows, they gradually release some and face off.”

Knowing a little is much better than knowing nothing.

“Good. Then go back home.”

“...Home?”

“Auyen will guide you.”

After locking subordinate G back in the basement, we held one final meeting.

“You look like you’ve made a decision.”

It wasn’t a tone asking because they really didn’t know, so I answered without hesitation.

“We free the hostages and escape this city together.”

Amelia asked briefly, and I answered immediately without hesitation.

“We figure it out from now.”

“...”

“All together.”

We judged it feasible and decided, but hadn’t made detailed plans yet.

It’s better to brainstorm with everyone rather than me deciding ~Nоvеlight~ alone.

Which prison to target first, which route to take.

Whether to climb the city wall or move through the sewers.

If we choose the wall, which point is best.

We debated these long and slowly formed a rough outline.

During a quiet lull in the meeting, Misha cautiously spoke.

“Isn’t it... a bit strange?”

A muttering without subject.

Elwen frowned and asked sharply.

“Strange?”

Elwen didn’t object.

Honestly, this doubt Misha voiced was something we all shared to some degree.

In this scenario, Noirak can never win.

Even if they had tens of times more hostages, the royal family isn’t afraid to shed blood.

And...

‘Noirak must know that too.’

A doomed invasion.

So why did the Noirak do something so foolish?

Were they just unable to think that far?

No way.

“Targeting two districts simultaneously is suspicious. They could barely hold out against one.”

At this point, it’s reasonable to think there’s a secret plan.

“What about the hostage situation?”

“They need time to carry out their next plan.”

Two heads are better than one.

Gathered here exchanging opinions, we realized we had a time limit.

We don’t know how much time remains, but everyone was impressed that we must act quickly.

“Alright, is everyone clear on the plan?”

“Ah, I’m not—”

“It’s okay, Ainard. You just stay next to Versil.”

“Oh! Really?”

Yes. I didn’t expect him to memorize all this.

It’s best to stick the smart guy with the smart guy.

“Then preparations are done. Let’s go.”

We finished all the preparations and were about to leave.

Is there some DNA that never forgets oaths, even if everything else is forgotten?

“Bjorn! Hey! What are you going to do with the guy locked in the basement? Didn’t you swear to spare him?”

Ugh, why do I remember useless things like this?

I couldn’t easily break my oath in front of Ainard, who doesn’t know I’m a player, but it didn’t cause big problems.

Okay, Ainard is convinced.

Creak, boom!

Let’s go.

We left the ruined old house and moved as stealthily as possible, avoiding patrols.

From the patrols’ movements, it seemed our presence was still unknown.

By the way, I assigned Amelia as our guide.

She’s practically an expert in stealth and infiltration.

“As I heard, there aren’t many guards.”

The old temple of the Reatlas faith we often visited.

Also the starting point of today’s escape plan.

Well, for me, it was just waiting.

The special team of Amelia with stealth device, Misha, and long-range assassin Elwen left the building together.

When I went down and headed to the front gate, all the guards standing there were already knocked out.

Ssshh!

After Amelia’s exploration tools erased all traces, we entered the temple.

Thunk! Crash! Ssssh!

Following Amelia, we repeated the process.

But a long tail is bound to get caught.

She says noise, but I hear nothing.

...Not that I don’t believe her.

“Do it.”

Tsk, I hoped this would be quiet and quick.

As we reached the stairs leading down to the underground prison, troops stationed in the temple appeared and attacked.

Killing them all before going down would be an option, but not time-efficient.

“Yandel, we’ll handle this here; you go down.”

Leaving surface cleanup to comrades, I hurried down.

The underground prison was much larger than the surface area.

Crash!

I smashed his head with the hammer, and the imprisoned explorers realized what was happening.

‘...No keys?’

Unexpected, but I wasn’t flustered.

There’s no problem in this world that can’t be solved.

If you have enough strength.

Creak!

With some force, the iron bars bent like spoons.

The explorers came out, asking all sorts of questions.

Goodwill building is nice, but I didn’t have time to answer kindly.

Yeah, to do something big, first we strengthen ourselves.

「[Essence of Orc Hero] embedded in character’s soul has been removed.」

.

Removal of the essence.

For priests of bishop rank or higher, the ability to remove an essence is essential, but there is one condition.

It can only be done inside a ‘temple’...

This is why many of the Noirak who mistakenly consumed essences became ruined characters.

Removing an essence once requires risking one’s life.

Especially if one’s face is well-known.

“Whew...”

I swayed slightly and returned the blue dagger given by the priest.

I always felt a considerable emptiness each time I removed an essence. It wasn’t just due to stats decreasing.

It’s hard to explain in words...

‘A sensation as if part of my soul is breaking away.’

If I had to describe it, this would be close.

Now I understood why MP in this world is called soul energy.

Swish.

Hoping to fill the void with something else, I quickly took out test tubes from the pocket dimension.

Arbet and Uumdal.

And the customized essence combining the [Gigantism] of the Orc Hero.

I popped open the cap and poured it into my mouth like a potion, curious.

If this were a game—

How would such a synthesized monster be represented?

「[???? Essence] is seeping into the character’s soul.」

Hmm, well... it probably wasn’t just question marks carelessly displayed and done.

Setting that aside, I focused on the bodily changes I felt.

Among explorers, there was a slang called ‘taming.’

The more one focuses on bodily changes while consuming essence, the easier it is to adapt to new abilities.

「Strength +100 increases.」

A vitality that could lift anything filled my body.

Of course, compared to an ogre, this strength was nothing...

「Jump power +120 increases.」

My thigh muscles tightened.

「Physical resistance +80 increases.」

My already tough skin became even tougher.

「Hearing +60 increases.」

Sounds scattered in the air became clearer.

These were the main noticeable immediate changes no matter how much I concentrated.

But this was not the end.

「Dark resistance +40 increases.」

「Soul energy +50 increases.」

「Natural regeneration +80 increases.」

「Endurance +60 increases.」

「Bone strength +60 increases.」

「Absorption +60 increases.」

Ogres have very high base stats, but in total ability score, Arbet couldn’t be matched.

Of course, Arbet’s drawback was his skill trap...

But since I stored only stats in essence using the underground 1st floor system, that didn’t apply to me.

“Phew...”

Having almost adapted to the stat changes, I cautiously opened the pocket dimension.

By the way, I had placed all the Numbers items I was wearing into the pocket dimension because of Uumdal’s passive skill’s characteristic.

[Soul Link] – The first acquired equipment becomes bound and grants additional abilities.

The first equipment obtained.

Though labeled so in-game, in reality this passive only works on ‘Numbers Items.’

Also...

‘If linked wrongly, it can’t be undone.’

Linking is possible only once, so Uumdal’s essence must always be handled carefully.

If consumed while fully equipped, one random item becomes bound...

Swish.

I reached into the pocket dimension and imagined specifically what to take out.

My hand closed around a large, solid ‘thing.’

‘No.87 Kraul’s Demon Crusher.’

...Of course, it wasn’t that.

Though the Demon Crusher is a high-performance weapon and choosing it would also make me strong.

But...

‘Not worth spending a passive slot on.’

There are countless passive skills suitable for shield barbarians. Normally I would have inserted one of those.

If only I had not had this at hand.

「Character equipped No.3 Aegis Barrier.」

「Total item level increases by +18,600.」

The ultimate shield normally only obtainable much later.

“Guidance of the soul.”

“No.3 Aegis Barrier is permanently bound to the character.”

“Bound equipment cannot be destroyed.”

“All effects of the bound equipment are increased by 10%.”

“Upon defeating monsters, the bound equipment gains individual experience, and at a certain level, unlocks unique effects.”

This shield grows from now on.

[Soul Link].

A mandatory skill when raising a shield dwarf synergizing with Numbers Items.

I had naturally done extensive research and knew almost all unique effects of high-tier equipment.

‘Unique effect unlocked at level 9.’

Even without unlocking, it was a very good skill.

Item performance increased 10% per level.

‘Still, having overlapping indestructible options is a bit wasteful...’

But since most high-tier Numbers have indestructible options, it’s not much of a loss.

The binding option... since I don’t plan to sell, it’s not a downside.

‘Gigantism.’

After confirming the active skill works well, I glanced around.

In theory, perfectly covering the fighting spirit lost due to Hiphramagent’s essence.

Yes, this is a battle roar, this is a barbarian.

“We’ll talk about that later. For now, we must move on to the next step before time runs out.”

He should have been more excited after consuming the new essence.

“...Fine.”

Anyway, Amelia was right that there was no time °• N o v e l i g h t •° to waste, so I hurried to the next plan.

Before raiding another prison, there was a prior task.

Feeling the sign of my speech beginning, everyone quietly looked at me.

Having lived as a barbarian, I knew I had a talent for speeches.

Amelia said I was just naturally gifted.

All reasonable doubts.

‘Now.’

No need for long speeches here.

At that, I raised my clenched fist, and the awaited cheer erupted.

“Waaaaaah!!”

Was it because my fame grew?

Or was it that I got better at provoking such cheers?

Either way, it felt easier to get those roars day by day.

‘Anyway, with this performance, no one will doubt my sincerity later...’

Contrasting with my passionate speech, I coldly scanned the crowd, then raised my voice again.

“But!”

Thinking the speech was starting again, the explorers kept quiet and listened.

“The enemies are strong! The rats grew by stealing our stores, and driving them out will cost us blood as well!”

Which isn’t a bad thing...

But it was time for the main topic.

“Clear throat! I recognize your courage and spirit. But! As a Lapdonian noble, warrior, explorer, and one who loves this city more than anyone else, I hope as little blood is spilled as possible. I want you to survive and return to your families.”

The speech was just groundwork for now.

“So, I, Bjorn, Yandel’s son, have made a decision.”

“From now on, I will distribute essences to you!!”

There’s that famous saying about growing stronger the more you share.

This was exactly that case.

“...Huh?”

The explorers’ expressions twisted oddly at the offer of free essences.

Understandable.

They must have wondered why that barbarian was spouting such madness.

Even my comrades didn’t react much differently.

“If you’re giving out essences...”

“You mean the ones obtained from the first basement floor?”

Only Amelia remained calm.

That’s because Amelia knew.

That this free distribution would ultimately bring me great benefit.

Of course, not simply because it would reduce loot going to the royal family, who are potential enemies.

Think about it.

‘If I just hand out essences recklessly here, how would we know how many essences remain?’

Simply put, by throwing away worthless essences to the explorers, I could secretly pocket the valuable ones later.

There’s no way for the royal family to find out we only distributed the cheap essences.

Saying this sounds a bit scummy, but...

‘Casualties during war are inevitable.’

How else would they investigate?

How would they know if the dead got the cheap essences or the expensive ones?

The justification is clean.

It was an investment to save the city.

‘Yeah, in war, things just disappear and get lost.’

The technical term is ‘shucking.’

Interestingly, the word’s origin is Japanese.

Anyway, that’s not important...

“Come on, everyone! Gather here! Don’t push! There’s still plenty of essence!”

I started the distribution in earnest.

After we launched a surprise attack on the main temple of the Reatlas cult as our first target, we moved along the pre-planned route and rescued the explorers trapped in each city facility.

And then...

“Are you saying you’ll give us the essences?”

“Yes! If you all get even a bit stronger, the chances of saving this city will increase! Here, take them quickly!”

Every time we escaped, we distributed essences like that.

Ah, of course, we didn’t just hand them out to anyone. At each escape, we gave one essence to the highest-ranked explorer there.

At least that way, I could keep some face with the royal family (if you could call it that).

Besides, the essences we got from the first basement floor weren’t nearly enough to give to everyone.

“Okay, that means the essence distribution is over.”

Auyen and the other comrades, who knew nothing about my unconventional plan, belatedly voiced such concerns over my radical actions.

Well, that much is true.

Ordinary people might find this kind of “mistake” suspicious.

“Ah, why bother keeping records? Too much hassle.”

I’m a barbarian.

So it’s not strange for me to handle things in a barbarian way.

“If it’s troublesome, I can ask people and make records now...”

“Enough! When in the world would I have time to do that in the middle of all this?”

“But still...”

Ha, well.

“Auyen, I said it’s fine.”

Looks like he finally sensed that I have some hidden motive...

‘Seems he still doesn’t know exactly what I’m planning.’

Well, Amelia also was horrified over and over when she first heard my plan.

They said no one in this world would dare pull off such crazy stuff against the royal family except me.

“Bjoooorn! So when do we leave for the next destination? I want to fight soon...!! Woohooooo!!”

Just as we were about to resume movement, Ainard couldn’t hold back and started hurrying me.

‘...Maybe I should have dialed down his fighting spirit to half?’

After regaining his fighting spirit, Ainard seemed to feel ashamed of his dark past and became much more aggressive than before.

So maybe if I’d only put in half, he would have been at a normal person’s level...

‘Nah, that’s not a proper spear barbarian. Just a spear wielder.’

A barbarian is supposed to be like this.

Honestly, as the Barbarian Lord, I was very glad to see Ainard regain his hearty spirit.

So I changed Ainard’s position as well.

Originally, his mission was to follow Versil and protect the rear...

But now—

“Roar, Ainard.”

“Behel—laaaaaaaaaa!!!”

He’s just smashing everything he sees right now on the front line, following me.

Using his new skill skillfully.

[Ainard Pneline cast [Indomitable Spirit].]

[For a certain time, physical stats increase significantly.]

[All kinds of status effects restricting action are reduced by 80%.]

This newly acquired combat buff allowed Ainard to charge through the battlefield like a tank.

Although it’s not a complete CC immunity or removal skill...

An 80% reduction plus his natural resistance means most CCs are broken in the blink of an eye.

[Ainard Pneline cast [Focused Strike].]

However, the core skill of spear barbarians, [Focused Strike], has yet to show great power.

[For the next hit only, all effects of currently held piercing-type skills are applied.]

Well, he only has one piercing skill, the [Trident].

Since mana is precious, I advised him not to use it unless necessary, but Ainard showed no intention of listening.

How can he resist when the spear emits white light?

‘Once we solve the city’s problem, I need to buy and feed him more attack skills.’

Once he has about three piercing skills stacked, [Focused Strike] will exceed the power of a rank 5 skill.

Though its true value lies elsewhere.

‘Maybe this skill is also a conversion-type skill?’

The condition for [Focused Strike] is ‘piercing type.’

Therefore, skills from other classes that can only be used with ranged weapons like bows can also be absorbed and used.

That’s why [Focused Strike] was chosen as the core essence for spear barbarians.

“Wooooooo!!”

Anyway, regardless of not being able to fully use [Focused Strike], Ainard ran across the battlefield showing a presence greater than even his days as the ‘Explosive Sword.’

This was because his raw stats had risen tremendously with this upgrade...

[Ainard Pneline has used attack skills 10 times.]

[Due to passive skill [War Machine], damage received is greatly reduced temporarily.]

[Ainard Pneline failed to defend.]

[Due to passive skill [Endurance], all resistances increase slightly.]

Thanks to defense passives, his combat stability improved drastically.

Meanwhile...

Thud!

His damage also rose significantly.

While he might have been better off before against many weak enemies, his single-target damage is on a completely different level.

After all, the Shingok Geochang spear is a top-tier spear weapon.

Stabbing it roughly can easily pierce even sturdy armor like tofu...

“When did the Explosive Sword start using a spear?”

“But it seems even stronger than when he used a sword...”

Ainard earned a new nickname.

[Ainard Pneline’s fame increased by +1.]

[......]
